<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Every invertible mixing probability-preserving transformation is mixing of every finite order. Thus Rokhlin's multiple-mixing problem for a single transformation has an affirmative answer.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Every invertible mixing probability-preserving transformation is mixing of every finite order. More explicitly, under the assumptions above, for every $k\ge3$ and every $A_1,\ldots,A_k\in\mathcal F$, $$\mu\bigl(A_1\cap T^{-n_1}A_2\cap\cdots\cap T^{-(n_1+\cdots+n_{k-1})}A_k\bigr) \longrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^k\mu(A_i)$$ as $\min(n_1,\ldots,n_{k-1})\to\infty$ through positive integers. No standardness or countable-generation assumption is imposed on the original probability space.
  - Explanation: Ordinary mixing implies every finite order of mixing for an invertible probability-preserving transformation, without a standardness assumption on the original space.
- **C2** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-zero-entropy-witness)): Suppose an invertible mixing transformation fails to be mixing of every order. There then exist an integer $k\geq3$, a finite alphabet $\mathcal A$, a shift-invariant probability measure $\mu_X$ on $X=\mathcal A^{\mathbb Z}$, and real one-site functions $f_1,\ldots,f_k$ such that the shift $T_X$ is mixing of every order less than $k$, $$\int f_i\,\mathrm d\mu_X=0,\qquad \lVert f_i\rVert_{\infty}\leq1, \qquad \lim_{n\to\infty}\frac1nH_{\mu_X}(x_1,\ldots,x_n)=0.$$ Moreover, there are $\delta>0$ and integer layouts $$0=d_1(b)<d_2(b)<\cdots<d_k(b),\qquad \min_{i<k}\bigl(d_{i+1}(b)-d_i(b)\bigr)\longrightarrow\infty,$$ for which $$\label{eq:witness-correlation} \int_X\prod_{i=1}^k f_i(T_X^{d_i(b)}x)\,\mathrm d\mu_X(x)\geq\delta \quad\text{for every }b.$$
  - Explanation: A hypothetical least failure yields a zero-entropy finite-alphabet mixing factor with centered bounded functions and a persistent product-correlation witness.
- **C3** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-saturation)): Every array object has an extension with the following property. For every $n\geq1$, every $F\in L^2(m_n)$, and each $$\mathcal D\in\{\mathcal P_n\}\cup \{\mathcal G_{n,j},\mathcal O_{n,j}:j\in I\},$$ no extension increases the norm of $\mathbb E[F\mid\mathcal D]$ after pullback. More precisely, if $\theta_n$ is the coordinatewise factor map from any extension and $\mathcal D'$ is the corresponding data in that extension, then $$\label{eq:saturation-equality} \mathbb E[F\circ\theta_n\mid\mathcal D'] =\mathbb E[F\mid\mathcal D]\circ\theta_n.$$
  - Explanation: An extension can be chosen so that richer admissible parameter and outside-plane data do not improve conditional expectations of old tests.
- **C4** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-local-conditioning)): Let $E,O_1,\ldots,O_s,B$ be standard random elements. Suppose $E_j=e_j(E)$ and $A_j=a_j(E_j,O_j)$ are measurable, and that $$\mathop{\mathrm{Law}}((O_j)_{j=1}^s\mid E) =\bigotimes_{j=1}^s K_j(E_j,\cdot),\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Law}}(B\mid E,O_1,\ldots,O_s)=L(E,A_1,\ldots,A_s;\cdot).$$ If $Q_j$ is the conditional law of $O_j$ given $(E_j,A_j)$, then $$\label{eq:local-conditioning} \mathop{\mathrm{Law}}((O_j)_{j=1}^s\mid E,A_1,\ldots,A_s,B) =\bigotimes_{j=1}^s Q_j(E_j,A_j;\cdot).$$
  - Explanation: Local summaries preserve the conditional product law when the auxiliary observation depends on the original variables only through those summaries.
- **C5** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-rolled-extension)): Let $R\subset I$ satisfy $\|R\|\leq k-2$, and choose $a\in I\setminus R$. There is an extension of the saturated object whose local state is $Y'=Y^I$, with local law $m_1$, parameter $$\label{eq:rolled-parameter} u'((y_c)_{c\in I})=((u(y_c))_{c\in I},(y_c)_{c\in R}),$$ and factor map $(y_c)_{c\in I}\mapsto y_a$. Its level-$n$ law is $m_{n+1}$, with the first axis treated as internal to the local state, and its time transformation is componentwise $T_Y$.
  - Explanation: A first axis can be included in the local state while selected parent states enter its parameter, preserving the array-object requirements.
- **C6** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-square-locality)): The following properties hold in $m_2$, and can be imposed simultaneously on one conull set of full parameter arrays $E$.* 1. *Conditional on $E$, every proper family of columns is independent, with column $j$ having law $\lambda_{q_j}$. Every proper family of rows is independent, with row $a$ having law $\lambda_{p_a}$.* 2. *Let $R\subset I$, $\|R\|\leq k-2$. Given $E$ and the full parent rows $Y_{R,I}$, every proper family of columns is independent, and each column has law $\lambda_{q_j}$ conditioned on its own parent entries $Y_{R,j}$.* 3. *If $a\notin R$ and $j_0\in I$, then $$\label{eq:single-cell-locality} \mathop{\mathrm{Law}}(y_{a,j_0}\mid E,Y_{R,I},Y_{I,I\setminus\{j_0\}}) =\lambda_{p_a}(\,\mathrm dy_{a,j_0}\mid Y_{a,I\setminus\{j_0\}}).$$* *There is also the following grouped form. Partition the rows into three nonempty groups $P,A,B$ and the columns into three nonempty groups $r,s,t$. At a fixed typical $E$, each grouped row $$(Y_{D,r},Y_{D,s},Y_{D,t}),\quad D\in\{P,A,B\},$$ has a three-coordinate law with pairwise independent coordinates, and the same holds for each grouped column. Given $Y_{P,I}$, any two grouped columns are independent with their own column laws conditioned on their entries in $P$. Finally, putting $\Lambda_A=\bigotimes_{a\in A}\lambda_{p_a}$, we have $$\label{eq:grouped-cell-locality} \mathop{\mathrm{Law}}(Y_{A,t}\mid E,Y_{P,I},Y_{I,r\cup s}) =\Lambda_A(\,\mathrm dY_{A,t}\mid Y_{A,r},Y_{A,s}).$$ All these grouped assertions hold for every ordering of the three row groups and the three column groups.
  - Explanation: Saturation supplies conditional row independence, parent-conditioned column independence and own-row conditional kernels in the ordered square.
- **C7** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-parameter-geometry)): In the level-two parameter law, for every $a\in I$ and every proper set $J\subset I$, $$\label{eq:parameter-geometry} \mathop{\mathrm{Law}}((q_j)_{j\in J}\mid p_a) =\bigotimes_{j\in J}K_a(p_a[j],\,\mathrm dq_j).$$ In particular, for each $i\in I$, the diagonal row and column tuples $p_i,q_i$ are conditionally independent, with the same law $K_i(u,\cdot)$, given their common coordinate $u=u_{i,i}$.
  - Explanation: Proper families of column parameter tuples are conditionally independent given a row tuple, with kernels determined by their shared entries.
- **C8** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-exact-lines)): Suppose $m\ge3$ and every $m-1$ coordinates of $\lambda$ are independent. Distinct exact lines at any coordinate are orthogonal. An exact line at one coordinate has a unique tuple of partner lines at the other coordinates. The exact tuples of lines form an abelian group under coordinatewise multiplication, and projection to any one coordinate is a group isomorphism onto its group of exact lines. Each of these groups is countable when the marginal $L^2$ spaces are separable. Every nonconstant exact line has zero mean.
  - Explanation: Proper-coordinate independence forces orthogonality of distinct exact lines, unique matching of partners and a countable abelian group of lines.
- **C9** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-rectangle)): For the grouped square just described, almost surely in $(z,c,d,x,y)$, $$\label{eq:rectangle-identities} L_A(x)J_t(d)=J_s(c)L_B(y),\qquad L_A(x)^*J_s(c)=J_t(d)L_B(y)^*.$$ The identities also hold in every ordering of the row and column groups.
  - Explanation: The conditional Markov operators satisfy both forward and adjoint rectangle identities under the grouped square locality assumptions.
- **C10** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-active-reduction)): In every grouped cell, the projections $P^V$ and $P^W$ commute. Every row Markov operator intertwines the column-active projections at its source and target; every column Markov operator intertwines the row-active projections. Put $$D_{i,l}=V_{i,l}\cap W_{i,l},\qquad P^D_{i,l}=P^V_{i,l}P^W_{i,l}.$$ Each $D_{i,l}$ is invariant under conjugation, and $P^D_{i,l}$ commutes with conjugation. All incident row and column operators intertwine the $P^D$ projections. For either choice of source in a row or column, the restrictions to $D$ have essential closed range span equal to the target $D$; the same holds for the reverse operators.
  - Explanation: Row-active and column-active projections commute; incident operators preserve their common ranges and retain essential range completeness there.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-fixed-squares)): For the four restricted families in <a href="#eq:restricted-rectangle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:restricted-rectangle">[eq:restricted-rectangle]</a>, each source square and target square is independent of its parameter almost surely, and each is injective on its $D$ space. The four polar parts are measurable families of unitaries between the $D$ spaces and satisfy the first rectangle identity.
  - Explanation: The restricted operator squares are fixed and injective, and their unitary polar parts retain the forward rectangle identity.
- **C12** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-polar-group)): There are fixed unitary identifications of the four $D$ spaces with one separable Hilbert space $\mathcal H$ such that the four polar variables in <a href="#eq:polar-rectangle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:polar-rectangle">[eq:polar-rectangle]</a> take values in one closed abelian subgroup $\mathcal G$ of the unitary group of $\mathcal H$. Their common support is $\mathcal G$. The variables $a,b$ have a common law $\rho$, invariant under left multiplication by every element of $\mathcal G$, and there is a measurable $\mathcal G$-valued function $h(z)$ such that $$\label{eq:corner-ratio} a(x)b(y)^{-1}=c_0(c)d_0(d)^{-1}=h(z).$$ The group $\mathcal G$ has a simultaneous orthonormal eigenbasis. Monomials in its diagonal circle coordinates, evaluated at $h(z)$, span $L^2(\sigma(h(z)))$ and are exact functions for both the row and column three-cell laws at $(P,r)$.
  - Explanation: Fixed identifications place the polar parts in a common abelian unitary group whose characters give simultaneous row and column exact functions.
- **C13** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-contraction-completeness)): In any three-cell row of the grouped square, abbreviate the cell spaces by $H_r,H_s,H_t$ and their common-active projections by $P_r,P_s,P_t$. For $g\in H_s$, $h\in H_t$, set $$C_r(g,h)=\mathbb E[g(Y_s)h(Y_t)\mid Y_r].$$ Then $$\label{eq:contraction-projections} P_rC_r(g,h)=C_r(g,P_th)=C_r(P_sg,h)=C_r(P_sg,P_th).$$ The contractions with both inputs in their $D$ spaces lie in $D_r$ and span it. The same assertions hold for each grouped column. Moreover, with $h(z)$ from Lemma <a href="#lem:polar-group" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:polar-group">14</a>, $$\label{eq:corner-completeness} D_{P,r}=L^2(\sigma(h(z))).$$ Consequently the intersection at every grouped cell is spanned by lines exact simultaneously for its three-cell row and column laws.
  - Explanation: Tensor-contraction projection identities prove that the simultaneous exact functions exhaust the common active space.
- **C14** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-common-exact-lines)): For almost every parameter square $E$, the following assertions hold simultaneously at all individual cells.* 1. *The orthogonal projections onto $V_{a,j}$ and $W_{a,j}$ commute. Their common range $V_{a,j}\cap W_{a,j}$ is spanned by the lines exact for both the full $k$-coordinate row law $\lambda_{p_a}$ and the full $k$-coordinate column law $\lambda_{q_j}$.* 2. *For distinct columns $s,t$, let $L^a_{s\leftarrow t}(v)$ be the row Markov operator from cell $(a,t)$ to $(a,s)$, conditioned on the remaining $k-2$ row entries $v$. Then, for almost every $v$, $$P^W_{a,s}L^a_{s\leftarrow t}(v) =L^a_{s\leftarrow t}(v)P^W_{a,t}.$$ Column operators likewise intertwine the corresponding row-active projections.* 3. *If $(\chi_j)_{j\in I}$ is an exact tuple for row $a$, with $\prod_j\chi_j(Y_{a,j})=c$, then $$\label{eq:exact-partner-transport} L^a_{s\leftarrow t}(v)\chi_t =c\!\prod_{j\ne s,t}\overline{\chi_j(v_j)}\, \overline{\chi_s}.$$ In particular, membership of one partner line in the column-active space is equivalent to membership of any other partner line in its column-active space. The analogous assertion holds with rows and columns exchanged.
  - Explanation: The grouped conclusions descend to individual cells, including projection commutation, full-law exact lines and transport of partner membership.
- **C15** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-active-exact)): For $\pi$-almost every $p$ and every $i\in I$, $$\label{eq:active-exact} \mathop{\mathrm{Ran}}\Pi_i(p)=C_i(p).$$
  - Explanation: Almost every tuple active space is the closed span of its exact lines.
- **C16** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-measurable-labels)): There is a measurable countable family of abelian groups $u\mapsto\mathcal L(u)$, defined on a $\nu$-conull set, with the following properties.* 1. *Each $\ell\in\mathcal L(u)$ labels a line in $H_u$ with a measurable modulus-one representative $\chi_{u,\ell}$. The identity label $0$ has representative $1$. Distinct labels are orthogonal, every nonidentity representative has mean zero, and $$\chi_{u,\ell}\chi_{u,\ell'} \in\mathbb T\chi_{u,\ell+\ell'}.$$* 2. *For $\pi$-almost every $p$, the exact lines at slot $i$ of $\lambda_p$ are precisely $\mathcal L(p_i)$, for every $i$, and they span its active space. Unique exact matching gives measurable group isomorphisms $$\Phi_{st}(p):\mathcal L(p_s)\longrightarrow\mathcal L(p_t) \qquad(s,t\in I),$$ where $\Phi_{ss}(p)=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}$.* 3. *Local time gives measurable group isomorphisms $$\tau_u:\mathcal L(u)\longrightarrow\mathcal L(T_Uu), \qquad [\chi]\longmapsto[\chi\circ T_Y^{-1}].$$ They respect exact matching. All local time assertions may be imposed on an invariant conull set.* 4. *For $\vartheta$-almost every parameter square, prescribing a label at any one cell gives a unique array of labels matched along every row and every column.
  - Explanation: Exact lines descend measurably to local parameter fibres as countable abelian groups, with time-equivariant matching consistent around the square.
- **C17** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-counting-lift)): There are sigma-finite measures $\widetilde\pi$ on $Z^I$ and $\widetilde\vartheta$ on $Z^{I\times I}$, obtained by counting matched label tuples and arrays over $\pi$ and $\vartheta$, respectively. Every coordinate marginal is $\xi$, and each row and column marginal of $\widetilde\vartheta$ is $\widetilde\pi$. For $s\ne t$ the coordinate transition in $\widetilde\pi$ is the probability kernel $$\label{eq:label-transition-kernel} K_{st}((u,\ell),A)= \int\mathbf1_A\bigl(p_t,\Phi_{st}(p)\ell\bigr) \,\pi(\,\mathrm dp\mid p_s=u).$$ Its operator $M_{st}$ on $L^2(\xi)$ is a contraction, has adjoint $M_{ts}$, and commutes with $V F=F\circ\tau$. For all $a\ne b$, $$\label{eq:label-square-identity} M_{st}=M_{ab}^*M_{st}M_{ab}.$$ Given a labelled row in $\widetilde\vartheta$, any proper family of labelled columns has the product of the column laws conditioned on their respective shared labelled points.
  - Explanation: Counting matched labels produces sigma-finite laws and contraction transition operators with time commutation and a square identity.
- **C18** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-blocks)): There are a countable index set $\mathcal B$, measurable partitions $$\mathcal L(u)=\bigsqcup_{b\in\mathcal B}B_b(u) \quad\text{for }\nu\text{-almost every }u,$$ and permutations $D_0,S_0$ of $\mathcal B$ with these properties. Every $B_b(u)$ has a fixed finite positive cardinality $m_b$, independent of $u$. For one fixed pair $s\ne t$, exact matching satisfies $$\Phi_{st}(p)B_b(p_s)=B_{D_0b}(p_t) \quad(\pi\text{-almost every }p),$$ and time satisfies $$\tau_u B_b(u)=B_{S_0b}(T_Uu).$$ Moreover, there are a countable abelian group $\mathcal L$, a partition $\mathcal L=\bigsqcup_b B_b$ into sets of cardinalities $m_b$, block-preserving group isomorphisms $J_u:\mathcal L\to\mathcal L(u)$ for typical $u$, and an automorphism $G$ of $\mathcal L$ such that $$\label{eq:abstract-block-time} G^r B_b=B_{S_0^{-r}b}\qquad(r\in\mathbb Z).$$ The isomorphisms $J_u$ are only asserted to exist separately in each fibre; they need not be chosen measurably or intertwine individual time trajectories.
  - Explanation: The local groups admit finite blocks of fixed cardinality; one abstract automorphism realizes the time permutation of whole blocks.
- **C19** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-block-witness)): Let the saturated array object lie over the zero-entropy finite-alphabet mixing process $(X,\mu_X,T_X)$ supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:zero-entropy-witness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:zero-entropy-witness">2</a>, with its centered finite-valued one-site functions $f_1,\ldots,f_k$ and nonzero level-$1$ product integral. There exist a function $f\in\{f_1,\ldots,f_k\}$, a countable abelian group $\mathcal L$, an automorphism $G$ of $\mathcal L$, a finite nonempty set $B\subset\mathcal L$, and a number $c>0$ with the following properties.* *For $\nu$-almost every $u$, the probability space $(Y,\eta_u)$ has factor map $x:Y\to X$ with $x_*\eta_u=\mu_X$, and admits representatives $(\chi_\ell^u)_{\ell\in\mathcal L}$ such that $$\begin{gathered} \chi_0^u=1,\qquad \|\chi_\ell^u\|=1,\qquad \chi_\ell^u\chi_{\ell'}^u\in\mathbb T\chi_{\ell+\ell'}^u, \label{eq:block-character-laws}\\ \int\chi_\ell^u\,\mathrm d\eta_u=0\quad(\ell\ne0),\qquad \langle \chi_\ell^u,\chi_{\ell'}^u\rangle_{L^2(\eta_u)} =\mathbf1_{\{\ell=\ell'\}}. \nonumber \end{gathered}$$ For every $r\ge0$ the function $$\label{eq:block-witness-sum} s_r(u)=\sum_{\ell\in B} \left\|\int f(T_X^r x(y))\, \overline{\chi_{G^r\ell}^u(y)}\,\mathrm d\eta_u(y)\right\|$$ has a measurable version and satisfies $$\label{eq:block-witness-integral} 0\le s_r(u)\le \|B\|\lVert f\rVert_\infty, \qquad \int_U s_r(u)\,\mathrm d\nu(u)=c>0.$$ The group identifications used to choose the representatives in <a href="#eq:block-character-laws" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:block-character-laws">[eq:block-character-laws]</a> need not be measurable in $u$. Their finite sums in <a href="#eq:block-witness-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:block-witness-sum">[eq:block-witness-sum]</a> are intrinsic sums over the measurable moving blocks. In particular, no time invariance of an individual $\eta_u$ is asserted or required.
  - Explanation: A finite moving label block retains a positive integrated coefficient sum, while every fibre has the original process as its marginal.
- **C20** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-orbit-cancellation)): Assume that $X$ is a mixing two-sided finite-alphabet stationary process whose alphabet partition has entropy rate zero. For every centered finite-valued one-site function $f$ on $X$, every probability coupling $x:Y\to X$, every family satisfying <a href="#eq:projective-characters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:projective-characters">[eq:projective-characters]</a>, every automorphism $G$, and every $l\in L$, one has $$\label{eq:orbit-cancellation} \lim_{N\to\infty}\frac1N\sum_{r=0}^{N-1} \left\|\int f(T_X^r x)\overline{\chi_{G^r l}}\,\mathrm d\eta\right\|=0.$$
  - Explanation: For a zero-entropy mixing finite-alphabet process, absolute correlations with automorphism-orbit characters have vanishing Cesàro mean under arbitrary coupling.
- **C21** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-entropy-cancellation)): Suppose <a href="#eq:projective-characters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:projective-characters">[eq:projective-characters]</a> holds and $(l_m)$ is dissociated. Let $(F_m)_{m\ge0}$ be complex random variables on $(Y,\eta)$ taking values in a fixed finite subset of the closed unit disk. If the Shannon entropy $H_n$ of $(F_0,\ldots,F_{n-1})$ satisfies $H_n=o(n)$, then $$\frac1n\sum_{m<n}\left\|\int F_m\overline{\chi_{l_m}}\,\mathrm d\eta\right\| \longrightarrow0.$$ No independence between the variables $F_m$ and the functions $\chi_{l_m}$ is assumed.
  - Explanation: Sublinear word entropy forces cancellation against dissociated projective characters without an independence assumption on the coupling.
- **C22** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-orbit-alternatives)): Let $G$ be an automorphism of an abelian group $L$ and $l\in L$. At least one of the following holds:* 1. *The sequence $(G^r l)_{r\ge0}$ is dissociated.* 2. *There is $p\ge1$ such that $(G^{pm+j}l)_{m\ge0}$ is dissociated for every $0\le j<p$.* 3. *There are $p,d\ge1$ such that, on the subgroup $K=\langle G^r l:r\in\mathbb Z\rangle$, the endomorphism $N=G^p-\mathop{\mathrm{id}}$ satisfies $N^d=0$. In this case $$\label{eq:binomial-orbit} G^{pm+j}l =\sum_{a=0}^{d-1}\binom ma N^aG^j l \qquad(m\ge0,\ 0\le j<p).$$
  - Explanation: An abelian-group automorphism orbit is dissociated, dissociated on arithmetic progressions, or polynomial on arithmetic progressions after a nilpotent difference reduction.
- **C23** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-polynomial-cancellation)): Let $(X,\mu_X,T_X)$ be mixing, let $x:Y\to X$ have law $\mu_X$, and assume <a href="#eq:projective-characters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:projective-characters">[eq:projective-characters]</a>. For every bounded centered function $F$ on $X$, every $p\ge1$, and every group-valued polynomial $P(m)$, one has $$\frac1n\sum_{m<n} \left\|\int F(T_X^{pm}x)\overline{\chi_{P(m)}}\,\mathrm d\eta\right\| \longrightarrow0.$$ Here neither zero entropy nor invariance of $\eta$ is required.
  - Explanation: Mixing alone forces cancellation against group-valued polynomial labels under arbitrary coupling, using induction and Hilbert-space differencing.
- **C24** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-endomorphisms-flows)): Let $(\Omega,\mathcal F,\mu)$ be an arbitrary probability space.* 1. *Suppose $T:\Omega\to\Omega$ is measurable, preserves $\mu$, and $\mu(A\cap T^{-n}B)\to\mu(A)\mu(B)$ as $n\to+\infty$ for every $A,B\in\mathcal F$. Then, for every fixed $k\ge2$ and measurable $A_1,\ldots,A_k$, $$\mu\left(\bigcap_{i=1}^kT^{-t_i}A_i\right) \longrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^k\mu(A_i)$$ along nonnegative integer times $t_1<\cdots<t_k$ whose least successive gap tends to infinity. Only preimages under forward iterates are used.* 2. *Let $(S_t)_{t\in\mathbb R}$ be a probability-preserving group of invertible measurable transformations. Assume $\mu(A\cap S_t^{-1}B)\to\mu(A)\mu(B)$ as $t\to+\infty$ for every $A,B\in\mathcal F$, and that its Koopman operators $U_t f=f\circ S_t$ are strongly continuous on $L^1(\mu)$; equivalently, $$\lVert U_s1_A-1_A\rVert_1\longrightarrow0\qquad(s\to0) \quad\text{for every }A\in\mathcal F.$$ The same product limit holds with $T^{-t_i}A_i$ replaced by $S_{t_i}^{-1}A_i$ and with arbitrary real times $t_1<\cdots<t_k$ whose least successive gap tends to infinity.
  - Explanation: Natural extensions give the endomorphism consequence; integer-time approximation gives the flow consequence under strong continuity on the stated function space.

Suppose $E_j=e_j(E)$ and $A_j=a_j(E_j,O_j)$ are measurable, and that $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Law}}((O_j)_{j=1}^s\\mid E)\n   =\\bigotimes_{j=1}^s K_j(E_j,\\cdot),\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Law}}(B\\mid E,O_1,\\ldots,O_s)=L(E,A_1,\\ldots,A_s;\\cdot).$$ If $Q_j$ is the conditional law of $O_j$ given $(E_j,A_j)$, then $$\\label{eq:local-conditioning}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Law}}((O_j)_{j=1}^s\\mid E,A_1,\\ldots,A_s,B)\n =\\bigotimes_{j=1}^s Q_j(E_j,A_j;\\cdot).$$",
      "explanation": "Local summaries preserve the conditional product law when the auxiliary observation depends on the original variables only through those summaries.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-square-locality",
        "exact_quote": "The following properties hold in $m_2$, and can be imposed simultaneously on one conull set of full parameter arrays $E$.*\n\n1.  *Conditional on $E$, every proper family of columns is independent, with column $j$ having law $\\lambda_{q_j}$. Every proper family of rows is independent, with row $a$ having law $\\lambda_{p_a}$.*\n\n2.  *Let $R\\subset I$, $|R|\\leq k-2$. Given $E$ and the full parent rows $Y_{R,I}$, every proper family of columns is independent, and each column has law $\\lambda_{q_j}$ conditioned on its own parent entries $Y_{R,j}$.*\n\n\n3.  *If $a\\notin R$ and $j_0\\in I$, then $$\\label{eq:single-cell-locality}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{Law}}(y_{a,j_0}\\mid E,Y_{R,I},Y_{I,I\\setminus\\{j_0\\}})\n     =\\lambda_{p_a}(\\,\\mathrm dy_{a,j_0}\\mid Y_{a,I\\setminus\\{j_0\\}}).$$*\n\n\n*There is also the following grouped form. Partition the rows into three nonempty groups $P,A,B$ and the columns into three nonempty groups $r,s,t$. At a fixed typical $E$, each grouped row $$(Y_{D,r},Y_{D,s},Y_{D,t}),\\quad D\\in\\{P,A,B\\},$$ has a three-coordinate law with pairwise independent coordinates, and the same holds for each grouped column. Given $Y_{P,I}$, any two grouped columns are independent with their own column laws conditioned on their entries in $P$. Finally, putting $\\Lambda_A=\\bigotimes_{a\\in A}\\lambda_{p_a}$, we have $$\\label{eq:grouped-cell-locality}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Law}}(Y_{A,t}\\mid E,Y_{P,I},Y_{I,r\\cup s})\n =\\Lambda_A(\\,\\mathrm dY_{A,t}\\mid Y_{A,r},Y_{A,s}).$$ All these grouped assertions hold for every ordering of the three row groups and the three column groups.",
        "source_id": "PM-145.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-parameter-geometry",
        "exact_quote": "In the level-two parameter law, for every $a\\in I$ and every proper set $J\\subset I$, $$\\label{eq:parameter-geometry}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Law}}((q_j)_{j\\in J}\\mid p_a)\n =\\bigotimes_{j\\in J}K_a(p_a[j],\\,\\mathrm dq_j).$$ In particular, for each $i\\in I$, the diagonal row and column tuples $p_i,q_i$ are conditionally independent, with the same law $K_i(u,\\cdot)$, given their common coordinate $u=u_{i,i}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-145.1"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-parameter-geometry",

          "source_id": "PM-145.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-exact-lines",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $m\\ge3$ and every $m-1$ coordinates of $\\lambda$ are independent. Distinct exact lines at any coordinate are orthogonal. An exact line at one coordinate has a unique tuple of partner lines at the other coordinates. The exact tuples of lines form an abelian group under coordinatewise multiplication, and projection to any one coordinate is a group isomorphism onto its group of exact lines. Each of these groups is countable when the marginal $L^2$ spaces are separable. Every nonconstant exact line has zero mean.",
        "source_id": "PM-145.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-active-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "In every grouped cell, the projections $P^V$ and $P^W$ commute. Every row Markov operator intertwines the column-active projections at its source and target; every column Markov operator intertwines the row-active projections. Put $$D_{i,l}=V_{i,l}\\cap W_{i,l},\\qquad\n P^D_{i,l}=P^V_{i,l}P^W_{i,l}.$$ Each $D_{i,l}$ is invariant under conjugation, and $P^D_{i,l}$ commutes with conjugation. All incident row and column operators intertwine the $P^D$ projections. For either choice of source in a row or column, the restrictions to $D$ have essential closed range span equal to the target $D$; the same holds for the reverse operators.",

        "exact_quote": "There are fixed unitary identifications of the four $D$ spaces with one separable Hilbert space $\\mathcal H$ such that the four polar variables in <a href=\"#eq:polar-rectangle\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:polar-rectangle\">[eq:polar-rectangle]</a> take values in one closed abelian subgroup $\\mathcal G$ of the unitary group of $\\mathcal H$. The variables $a,b$ have a common law $\\rho$, invariant under left multiplication by every element of $\\mathcal G$, and there is a measurable $\\mathcal G$-valued function $h(z)$ such that $$\\label{eq:corner-ratio}\n a(x)b(y)^{-1}=c_0(c)d_0(d)^{-1}=h(z).$$ The group $\\mathcal G$ has a simultaneous orthonormal eigenbasis.       "explanation": "Fixed identifications place the polar parts in a common abelian unitary group whose characters give simultaneous row and column exact functions.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-contraction-completeness",
        "exact_quote": "In any three-cell row of the grouped square, abbreviate the cell spaces by $H_r,H_s,H_t$ and their common-active projections by $P_r,P_s,P_t$. For $g\\in H_s$, $h\\in H_t$, set $$C_r(g,h)=\\mathbb E[g(Y_s)h(Y_t)\\mid Y_r].$$ Then $$\\label{eq:contraction-projections}\n P_rC_r(g,h)=C_r(g,P_th)=C_r(P_sg,h)=C_r(P_sg,P_th).$$ The contractions with both inputs in their $D$ spaces lie in $D_r$ and span it. The same assertions hold for each grouped column. Moreover, with $h(z)$ from Lemma <a href=\"#lem:polar-group\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:polar-group\">14</a>, $$\\label{eq:corner-completeness}\n D_{P,r}=L^2(\\sigma(h(z))).$$ Consequently the intersection at every grouped cell is spanned by lines exact simultaneously for its three-cell row and column laws.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-common-exact-lines",
        "exact_quote": "For almost every parameter square $E$, the following assertions hold simultaneously at all individual cells.*\n\n1.  *The orthogonal projections onto $V_{a,j}$ and $W_{a,j}$ commute. Their common range $V_{a,j}\\cap W_{a,j}$ is spanned by the lines exact for both the full $k$-coordinate row law $\\lambda_{p_a}$ and the full $k$-coordinate column law $\\lambda_{q_j}$.*\n\n2.  *For distinct columns $s,t$, let $L^a_{s\\leftarrow t}(v)$ be the row Markov operator from cell $(a,t)$ to $(a,s)$, conditioned on the remaining $k-2$ row entries $v$. Then, for almost every $v$, $$P^W_{a,s}L^a_{s\\leftarrow t}(v)\n     =L^a_{s\\leftarrow t}(v)P^W_{a,t}.$$ Column operators likewise intertwine the corresponding row-active projections.*\n\n\n3.  *If $(\\chi_j)_{j\\in I}$ is an exact tuple for row $a$, with $\\prod_j\\chi_j(Y_{a,j})=c$, then $$\\label{eq:exact-partner-transport}\n     L^a_{s\\leftarrow t}(v)\\chi_t\n     =c\\!\\prod_{j\\ne s,t}\\overline{\\chi_j(v_j)}\\,\n       \\overline{\\chi_s}.$$ In particular, membership of one partner line in the column-active space is equivalent to membership of any other partner line in its column-active space. The analogous assertion holds with rows and columns exchanged.",
        "source_id": "PM-145.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-common-exact-lines",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-measurable-labels",
        "exact_quote": "There is a measurable countable family of abelian groups $u\\mapsto\\mathcal L(u)$, defined on a $\\nu$-conull set, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Each $\\ell\\in\\mathcal L(u)$ labels a line in $H_u$ with a measurable modulus-one representative $\\chi_{u,\\ell}$. The identity label $0$ has representative $1$. Distinct labels are orthogonal, every nonidentity representative has mean zero, and $$\\chi_{u,\\ell}\\chi_{u,\\ell'}\n     \\in\\mathbb T\\chi_{u,\\ell+\\ell'}.$$*\n\n2.  *For $\\pi$-almost every $p$, the exact lines at slot $i$ of $\\lambda_p$ are precisely $\\mathcal L(p_i)$, for every $i$, and they span its active space. Unique exact matching gives measurable group isomorphisms $$\\Phi_{st}(p):\\mathcal L(p_s)\\longrightarrow\\mathcal L(p_t)\n     \\qquad(s,t\\in I),$$ where $\\Phi_{ss}(p)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}$.*\n\n3.  *Local time gives measurable group isomorphisms $$\\tau_u:\\mathcal L(u)\\longrightarrow\\mathcal L(T_Uu),\n     \\qquad [\\chi]\\longmapsto[\\chi\\circ T_Y^{-1}].$$ They respect exact matching. All local time assertions may be imposed on an invariant conull set.*\n\n4.  *For $\\vartheta$-almost every parameter square, prescribing a label at any one cell gives a unique array of labels matched along every row and every column.",
        "source_id": "PM-145.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

Unique exact matching gives measurable group isomorphisms $$\\Phi_{st}(p):\\mathcal L(p_s)\\longrightarrow\\mathcal L(p_t)\n     \\qquad(s,t\\in I),$$ where $\\Phi_{ss}(p)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}$.*\n\n3.  *Local time gives measurable group isomorphisms $$\\tau_u:\\mathcal L(u)\\longrightarrow\\mathcal L(T_Uu),\n     \\qquad [\\chi]\\longmapsto[\\chi\\circ T_Y^{-1}].$$ They respect exact matching. All local time assertions may be imposed on an invariant conull set.*\n\n4.  *For $\\vartheta$-almost every parameter square, prescribing a label at any one cell gives a unique array of labels matched along every row and every column.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-counting-lift",
        "exact_quote": "There are sigma-finite measures $\\widetilde\\pi$ on $Z^I$ and $\\widetilde\\vartheta$ on $Z^{I\\times I}$, obtained by counting matched label tuples and arrays over $\\pi$ and $\\vartheta$, respectively. Every coordinate marginal is $\\xi$, and each row and column marginal of $\\widetilde\\vartheta$ is $\\widetilde\\pi$. For $s\\ne t$ the coordinate transition in $\\widetilde\\pi$ is the probability kernel $$\\label{eq:label-transition-kernel}\n K_{st}((u,\\ell),A)=\n \\int\\mathbf1_A\\bigl(p_t,\\Phi_{st}(p)\\ell\\bigr)\n                   \\,\\pi(\\,\\mathrm dp\\mid p_s=u).$$ Its operator $M_{st}$ on $L^2(\\xi)$ is a contraction, has adjoint $M_{ts}$, and commutes with $V F=F\\circ\\tau$. For all $a\\ne b$, $$\\label{eq:label-square-identity}\n M_{st}=M_{ab}^*M_{st}M_{ab}.$$ Given a labelled row in $\\widetilde\\vartheta$, any proper family of labelled columns has the product of the column laws conditioned on their respective shared labelled points.",
        "source_id": "PM-145.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

For all $a\\ne b$, $$\\label{eq:label-square-identity}\n M_{st}=M_{ab}^*M_{st}M_{ab}.$$ Given a labelled row in $\\widetilde\\vartheta$, any proper family of labelled columns has the product of the column laws conditioned on their respective shared labelled points.",
          "source_id": "PM-145.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-blocks",
        "exact_quote": "There are a countable index set $\\mathcal B$, measurable partitions $$\\mathcal L(u)=\\bigsqcup_{b\\in\\mathcal B}B_b(u)\n \\quad\\text{for }\\nu\\text{-almost every }u,$$ and permutations $D_0,S_0$ of $\\mathcal B$ with these properties. Every $B_b(u)$ has a fixed finite positive cardinality $m_b$, independent of $u$. For one fixed pair $s\\ne t$, exact matching satisfies $$\\Phi_{st}(p)B_b(p_s)=B_{D_0b}(p_t)\n \\quad(\\pi\\text{-almost every }p),$$ and time satisfies $$\\tau_u B_b(u)=B_{S_0b}(T_Uu).$$ Moreover, there are a countable abelian group $\\mathcal L$, a partition $\\mathcal L=\\bigsqcup_b B_b$ into sets of cardinalities $m_b$, block-preserving group isomorphisms $J_u:\\mathcal L\\to\\mathcal L(u)$ for typical $u$, and an automorphism $G$ of $\\mathcal L$ such that $$\\label{eq:abstract-block-time}\n G^r B_b=B_{S_0^{-r}b}\\qquad(r\\in\\mathbb Z).$$ The isomorphisms $J_u$ are only asserted to exist separately in each fibre; they need not be chosen measurably or intertwine individual time trajectories.",
        "source_id": "PM-145.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-blocks",
          "exact_quote": "There are a countable index set $\\mathcal B$, measurable partitions $$\\mathcal L(u)=\\bigsqcup_{b\\in\\mathcal B}B_b(u)\n \\quad\\text{for }\\nu\\text{-almost every }u,$$ and permutations $D_0,S_0$ of $\\mathcal B$ with these properties. Every $B_b(u)$ has a fixed finite positive cardinality $m_b$, independent of $u$. For one fixed pair $s\\ne t$, exact matching satisfies $$\\Phi_{st}(p)B_b(p_s)=B_{D_0b}(p_t)\n \\quad(\\pi\\text{-almost every }p),$$ and time satisfies $$\\tau_u B_b(u)=B_{S_0b}(T_Uu).$$ Moreover, there are a countable abelian group $\\mathcal L$, a partition $\\mathcal L=\\bigsqcup_b B_b$ into sets of cardinalities $m_b$, block-preserving group isomorphisms $J_u:\\mathcal L\\to\\mathcal L(u)$ for typical $u$, and an automorphism $G$ of $\\mathcal L$ such that $$\\label{eq:abstract-block-time}\n G^r B_b=B_{S_0^{-r}b}\\qquad(r\\in\\mathbb Z).$$ The isomorphisms $J_u$ are only asserted to exist separately in each fibre; they need not be chosen measurably or intertwine individual time trajectories.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-block-witness",
        "exact_quote": "Let the saturated array object lie over the zero-entropy finite-alphabet mixing process $(X,\\mu_X,T_X)$ supplied by Proposition <a href=\"#prop:zero-entropy-witness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:zero-entropy-witness\">2</a>, with its centered finite-valued one-site functions $f_1,\\ldots,f_k$ and nonzero level-$1$ product integral. There exist a function $f\\in\\{f_1,\\ldots,f_k\\}$, a countable abelian group $\\mathcal L$, an automorphism $G$ of $\\mathcal L$, a finite nonempty set $B\\subset\\mathcal L$, and a number $c>0$ with the following properties.*\n\n\n\n\n*For $\\nu$-almost every $u$, the probability space $(Y,\\eta_u)$ has factor map $x:Y\\to X$ with $x_*\\eta_u=\\mu_X$, and admits representatives $(\\chi_\\ell^u)_{\\ell\\in\\mathcal L}$ such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n \\chi_0^u=1,\\qquad |\\chi_\\ell^u|=1,\\qquad\n \\chi_\\ell^u\\chi_{\\ell'}^u\\in\\mathbb T\\chi_{\\ell+\\ell'}^u,\n \\label{eq:block-character-laws}\\\\\n \\int\\chi_\\ell^u\\,\\mathrm d\\eta_u=0\\quad(\\ell\\ne0),\\qquad\n \\langle \\chi_\\ell^u,\\chi_{\\ell'}^u\\rangle_{L^2(\\eta_u)}\n       =\\mathbf1_{\\{\\ell=\\ell'\\}}.\n \\nonumber\n\\end{gathered}$$ For every $r\\ge0$ the function $$\\label{eq:block-witness-sum}\n s_r(u)=\\sum_{\\ell\\in B}\n \\left|\\int f(T_X^r x(y))\\,\n               \\overline{\\chi_{G^r\\ell}^u(y)}\\,\\mathrm d\\eta_u(y)\\right|$$ has a measurable version and satisfies $$\\label{eq:block-witness-integral}\n 0\\le s_r(u)\\le |B|\\lVert f\\rVert_\\infty,\n \\qquad \\int_U s_r(u)\\,\\mathrm d\\nu(u)=c>0.$$ The group identifications used to choose the representatives in <a href=\"#eq:block-character-laws\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:block-character-laws\">[eq:block-character-laws]</a> need not be measurable in $u$. Their finite sums in <a href=\"#eq:block-witness-sum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:block-witness-sum\">[eq:block-witness-sum]</a> are intrinsic sums over the measurable moving blocks. In particular, no time invariance of an individual $\\eta_u$ is asserted or required.",
        "source_id": "PM-145.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-block-witness",

Their finite sums in <a href=\"#eq:block-witness-sum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:block-witness-sum\">[eq:block-witness-sum]</a> are intrinsic sums over the measurable moving blocks.       "exact_statement": "Let the saturated array object lie over the zero-entropy finite-alphabet mixing process $(X,\\mu_X,T_X)$ supplied by Proposition <a href=\"#prop:zero-entropy-witness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:zero-entropy-witness\">2</a>, with its centered finite-valued one-site functions $f_1,\\ldots,f_k$ and nonzero level-$1$ product integral.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-orbit-cancellation",
        "exact_quote": "Assume that $X$ is a mixing two-sided finite-alphabet stationary process whose alphabet partition has entropy rate zero. For every centered finite-valued one-site function $f$ on $X$, every probability coupling $x:Y\\to X$, every family satisfying <a href=\"#eq:projective-characters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:projective-characters\">[eq:projective-characters]</a>, every automorphism $G$, and every $l\\in L$, one has $$\\label{eq:orbit-cancellation}\n \\lim_{N\\to\\infty}\\frac1N\\sum_{r=0}^{N-1}\n \\left|\\int f(T_X^r x)\\overline{\\chi_{G^r l}}\\,\\mathrm d\\eta\\right|=0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-145.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-orbit-cancellation",

          "source_id": "PM-145.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-cancellation",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose <a href=\"#eq:projective-characters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:projective-characters\">[eq:projective-characters]</a> holds and $(l_m)$ is dissociated. Let $(F_m)_{m\\ge0}$ be complex random variables on $(Y,\\eta)$ taking values in a fixed finite subset of the closed unit disk. If the Shannon entropy $H_n$ of $(F_0,\\ldots,F_{n-1})$ satisfies $H_n=o(n)$, then $$\\frac1n\\sum_{m<n}\\left|\\int F_m\\overline{\\chi_{l_m}}\\,\\mathrm d\\eta\\right|\n \\longrightarrow0.$$ No independence between the variables $F_m$ and the functions $\\chi_{l_m}$ is assumed.",
        "source_id": "PM-145.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-cancellation",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose <a href=\"#eq:projective-characters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:projective-characters\">[eq:projective-characters]</a> holds and $(l_m)$ is dissociated.           "source_id": "PM-145.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-orbit-alternatives",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be an automorphism of an abelian group $L$ and $l\\in L$. At least one of the following holds:*\n\n1.  *The sequence $(G^r l)_{r\\ge0}$ is dissociated.*\n\n2.  *There is $p\\ge1$ such that $(G^{pm+j}l)_{m\\ge0}$ is dissociated for every $0\\le j<p$.*\n\n\n3.  *There are $p,d\\ge1$ such that, on the subgroup $K=\\langle G^r l:r\\in\\mathbb Z\\rangle$, the endomorphism $N=G^p-\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}$ satisfies $N^d=0$. In this case $$\\label{eq:binomial-orbit}\n           G^{pm+j}l\n           =\\sum_{a=0}^{d-1}\\binom ma N^aG^j l\n           \\qquad(m\\ge0,\\ 0\\le j<p).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-145.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-orbit-alternatives",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be an automorphism of an abelian group $L$ and $l\\in L$. In this case $$\\label{eq:binomial-orbit}\n           G^{pm+j}l\n           =\\sum_{a=0}^{d-1}\\binom ma N^aG^j l\n           \\qquad(m\\ge0,\\ 0\\le j<p).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-145.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-polynomial-cancellation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(X,\\mu_X,T_X)$ be mixing, let $x:Y\\to X$ have law $\\mu_X$, and assume <a href=\"#eq:projective-characters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:projective-characters\">[eq:projective-characters]</a>.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-endomorphisms-flows",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(\\Omega,\\mathcal F,\\mu)$ be an arbitrary probability space.*\n\n1.  *Suppose $T:\\Omega\\to\\Omega$ is measurable, preserves $\\mu$, and $\\mu(A\\cap T^{-n}B)\\to\\mu(A)\\mu(B)$ as $n\\to+\\infty$ for every $A,B\\in\\mathcal F$. Then, for every fixed $k\\ge2$ and measurable $A_1,\\ldots,A_k$, $$\\mu\\left(\\bigcap_{i=1}^kT^{-t_i}A_i\\right)\n     \\longrightarrow\\prod_{i=1}^k\\mu(A_i)$$ along nonnegative integer times $t_1<\\cdots<t_k$ whose least successive gap tends to infinity. Only preimages under forward iterates are used.*\n\n2.  *Let $(S_t)_{t\\in\\mathbb R}$ be a probability-preserving group of invertible measurable transformations. Assume $\\mu(A\\cap S_t^{-1}B)\\to\\mu(A)\\mu(B)$ as $t\\to+\\infty$ for every $A,B\\in\\mathcal F$, and that its Koopman operators $U_t f=f\\circ S_t$ are strongly continuous on $L^1(\\mu)$; equivalently, $$\\lVert U_s1_A-1_A\\rVert_1\\longrightarrow0\\qquad(s\\to0)\n     \\quad\\text{for every }A\\in\\mathcal F.$$ The same product limit holds with $T^{-t_i}A_i$ replaced by $S_{t_i}^{-1}A_i$ and with arbitrary real times $t_1<\\cdots<t_k$ whose least successive gap tends to infinity.",
        "source_id": "PM-145.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-endomorphisms-flows",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(\\Omega,\\mathcal F,\\mu)$ be an arbitrary probability space.*\n\n1.  *Suppose $T:\\Omega\\to\\Omega$ is measurable, preserves $\\mu$, and $\\mu(A\\cap T^{-n}B)\\to\\mu(A)\\mu(B)$ as $n\\to+\\infty$ for every $A,B\\in\\mathcal F$. Then, for every fixed $k\\ge2$ and measurable $A_1,\\ldots,A_k$, $$\\mu\\left(\\bigcap_{i=1}^kT^{-t_i}A_i\\right)\n     \\longrightarrow\\prod_{i=1}^k\\mu(A_i)$$ along nonnegative integer times $t_1<\\cdots<t_k$ whose least successive gap tends to infinity. Only preimages under forward iterates are used.*\n\n2.  *Let $(S_t)_{t\\in\\mathbb R}$ be a probability-preserving group of invertible measurable transformations. Assume $\\mu(A\\cap S_t^{-1}B)\\to\\mu(A)\\mu(B)$ as $t\\to+\\infty$ for every $A,B\\in\\mathcal F$, and that its Koopman operators $U_t f=f\\circ S_t$ are strongly continuous on $L^1(\\mu)$; equivalently, $$\\lVert U_s1_A-1_A\\rVert_1\\longrightarrow0\\qquad(s\\to0)\n     \\quad\\text{for every }A\\in\\mathcal F.$$ The same product limit holds with $T^{-t_i}A_i$ replaced by $S_{t_i}^{-1}A_i$ and with arbitrary real times $t_1<\\cdots<t_k$ whose least successive gap tends to infinity.",

      "exact_statement": "Let $(\\Omega,\\mathcal F,\\mu)$ be an arbitrary probability space.*\n\n1.  *Suppose $T:\\Omega\\to\\Omega$ is measurable, preserves $\\mu$, and $\\mu(A\\cap T^{-n}B)\\to\\mu(A)\\mu(B)$ as $n\\to+\\infty$ for every $A,B\\in\\mathcal F$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **mixing** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The transformation is *mixing* if $$\mu(A\cap T^{-n}B)\longrightarrow\mu(A)\mu(B) \qquad(\|n\|\to\infty)$$ for every $A,B\in\mathcal F$.
- **mixing of order k** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For an integer $k\ge2$, it is *mixing of order $k$* if $$\mu\left(\bigcap_{i=1}^k T^{-t_i}A_i\right) \longrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^k\mu(A_i)$$ whenever $t_1<\cdots<t_k$ are integers and $\min_{i<k}(t_{i+1}-t_i)\to\infty$, for every fixed choice of the measurable sets $A_i$.
- **array object** ([source](#stmt-def-array-object)): An *array object* consists of standard probability systems $(Y,m_0,T_Y)$ and $(U,\nu,T_U)$, factor maps $$\kappa:Y\longrightarrow X,\qquad u:Y\longrightarrow U,$$ and probability laws $m_n$ on $Y^{I^n}$ for $n\geq0$. All transformations are invertible and the factor maps commute with time. The laws satisfy the following requirements. 1. Each $m_n$ is invariant under simultaneous $T_Y$ and projects coordinatewise to $\rho_n$. For $n\geq1$, fixing any axis at a slot gives $m_{n-1}$, and every proper family of last-axis planes is independent under $m_n$. 2. For $n\geq1$, conditional on the complete parameter array $(u(y_v))_{v\in I^n}$, every proper family of last-axis planes is independent. The conditional law of each plane is $m_{n-1}$ conditioned only on that plane’s own parameter array. 3. Under $m_0$, $\kappa(y)$ and $u(y)$ are independent; in particular, $\kappa_*m_0=\mu_X$. An *extension* is another such object together with a local factor map $\theta:Y'\to Y$ preserving every array law and the map to $X$, such that the old parameter is a factor of the new parameter. The latter means that $u\circ\theta=\psi\circ u'$ for a time-commuting factor $\psi:U'\to U$.
- **active space** ([source](#stmt-def-active-space)): The *active space* at coordinate $i$ is the closed subspace $$\mathcal A_i(\lambda) =\overline{\mathop{\mathrm{span}}}\bigl\{ \mathbb E_\lambda[F(Y_j:j\ne i)\mid Y_i]:F\text{ bounded and measurable} \bigr\}\subseteq L^2(\mu_i).$$
- **exact tuple** ([source](#stmt-def-active-space)): An *exact tuple* is a tuple of measurable functions $\chi_i:S_i\to\mathbb T$ for which $$\label{eq:exact-tuple} \prod_{i=1}^m\chi_i(Y_i)=c\qquad\lambda\text{-almost surely}, \qquad c\in\mathbb T.$$
- **exact line** ([source](#stmt-def-active-space)): An *exact line* at coordinate $i$ is a one-dimensional subspace $\mathbb C\chi_i$ occurring in an exact tuple. Representatives differing by a constant of modulus one define the same line.
- **dissociated sequence** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): A sequence $(l_m)_{m\ge0}$ in an abelian group is *dissociated* if $$\sum_{m\in A}\varepsilon_m l_m\ne0$$ for every finite nonempty set $A\subset\mathbb N\cup\{0\}$ and every choice $\varepsilon_m\in\{-1,1\}$.
- **group-valued polynomial** ([source](#pm-h-32)): A group-valued polynomial of degree at most $d$ on the nonnegative integers will mean a sequence $$P(m)=\sum_{a=0}^d\binom ma l_a,\qquad l_a\in L.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | least failing order | ≥ | $3$ | Ordinary mixing excludes a smaller least failure. |
| Q2 | supremum norm of a centered witness function | ≤ | $1$ | Witness functions obtained by centering functions with values in the unit interval. |
| Q3 | parent-set cardinality | ≤ | $k-2$ | A selected projection slot is outside the parent set. |
| Q4 | trace of a line-detection operator | ≤ | $1$ | The line-detection operator at a typical tuple parameter. |
| Q5 | exponential moment parameter | = | $\varepsilon/4$ | The tail estimate for a positive deviation in the unit interval. |
| Q6 | dissociated-sum tail probability | ≤ | $e^{-\varepsilon^2 n/8}$ | Positive deviations in the unit interval and a finite initial segment of labels. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-prop-zero-entropy-witness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-zero-entropy-witness): Past-tail replacement, entropy chain rule and finite coding. -> A zero-entropy finite-alphabet witness.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-prop-saturation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-saturation): Conditional-expectation energy approximation and sequential inverse limits. -> A saturated array object.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-square-locality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-square-locality): Rolling an axis and conditioning through local summaries. -> Exact square locality in every grouped orientation.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-common-exact-lines) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-common-exact-lines): Rectangle identities, polar normalization and tensor-contraction completeness. -> Common active spaces spanned by exact lines.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-prop-measurable-labels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-measurable-labels): Random-projection support, measurable spectral enumeration and descent to the local parameter. -> Measurable local abelian label groups with consistent square matchings.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-blocks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-blocks): Counting lift, deterministic transition information and finite-mass level sets. -> Finite blocks with a time permutation realized by an abstract automorphism.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-prop-block-witness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-block-witness): Projecting the correlation witness onto label lines and transporting intrinsic block sums. -> A moving finite block with a positive integrated coefficient sum.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-orbit-cancellation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-polynomial-cancellation-2): Dissociated exponential moments and entropy, orbit algebra, and polynomial Hilbert-space differencing. -> Vanishing average absolute orbit correlations.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-polynomial-cancellation-3): Fibrewise orbit cancellation and bounded convergence. -> A contradiction excluding every least failing finite order.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-cor-endomorphisms-flows) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-endomorphisms-flows): Natural extension of a finite-symbol factor and integer-time approximation with strong continuity. -> The endomorphism and flow consequences.

          "exact_quote": "Suppose an invertible mixing transformation fails to be mixing of every order. There then exist an integer $k\\geq3$, a finite alphabet $\\mathcal A$, a shift-invariant probability measure $\\mu_X$ on $X=\\mathcal A^{\\mathbb Z}$, and real one-site functions $f_1,\\ldots,f_k$ such that the shift $T_X$ is mixing of every order less than $k$, $$\\int f_i\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_X=0,\\qquad \\lVert f_i\\rVert_{\\infty}\\leq1,\n \\qquad\n \\lim_{n\\to\\infty}\\frac1nH_{\\mu_X}(x_1,\\ldots,x_n)=0.$$ Moreover, there are $\\delta>0$ and integer layouts $$0=d_1(b)<d_2(b)<\\cdots<d_k(b),\\qquad\n \\min_{i<k}\\bigl(d_{i+1}(b)-d_i(b)\\bigr)\\longrightarrow\\infty,$$ for which $$\\label{eq:witness-correlation}\n \\int_X\\prod_{i=1}^k f_i(T_X^{d_i(b)}x)\\,\\mathrm d\\mu_X(x)\\geq\\delta\n \\quad\\text{for every }b.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-145.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-saturation",
          "exact_quote": "Every array object has an extension with the following property. For every $n\\geq1$, every $F\\in L^2(m_n)$, and each $$\\mathcal D\\in\\{\\mathcal P_n\\}\\cup\n \\{\\mathcal G_{n,j},\\mathcal O_{n,j}:j\\in I\\},$$ no extension increases the norm of $\\mathbb E[F\\mid\\mathcal D]$ after pullback. More precisely, if $\\theta_n$ is the coordinatewise factor map from any extension and $\\mathcal D'$ is the corresponding data in that extension, then $$\\label{eq:saturation-equality}\n \\mathbb E[F\\circ\\theta_n\\mid\\mathcal D']\n =\\mathbb E[F\\mid\\mathcal D]\\circ\\theta_n.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-145.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "R2",
      "inputs": [
        "Ordered array laws retaining the correlation witness."
      ],
      "method": "Conditional-expectation energy approximation and sequential inverse limits.",
      "output": "A saturated array object.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-saturation"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-square-locality",
      "depends_on": [
        "R2"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-square-locality",
          "exact_quote": "The following properties hold in $m_2$, and can be imposed simultaneously on one conull set of full parameter arrays $E$.*\n\n1.  *Conditional on $E$, every proper family of columns is independent, with column $j$ having law $\\lambda_{q_j}$. Every proper family of rows is independent, with row $a$ having law $\\lambda_{p_a}$.*\n\n2.  *Let $R\\subset I$, $|R|\\leq k-2$. Given $E$ and the full parent rows $Y_{R,I}$, every proper family of columns is independent, and each column has law $\\lambda_{q_j}$ conditioned on its own parent entries $Y_{R,j}$.*\n\n\n3.  *If $a\\notin R$ and $j_0\\in I$, then $$\\label{eq:single-cell-locality}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{Law}}(y_{a,j_0}\\mid E,Y_{R,I},Y_{I,I\\setminus\\{j_0\\}})\n     =\\lambda_{p_a}(\\,\\mathrm dy_{a,j_0}\\mid Y_{a,I\\setminus\\{j_0\\}}).$$*\n\n\n*There is also the following grouped form. Partition the rows into three nonempty groups $P,A,B$ and the columns into three nonempty groups $r,s,t$. At a fixed typical $E$, each grouped row $$(Y_{D,r},Y_{D,s},Y_{D,t}),\\quad D\\in\\{P,A,B\\},$$ has a three-coordinate law with pairwise independent coordinates, and the same holds for each grouped column. Given $Y_{P,I}$, any two grouped columns are independent with their own column laws conditioned on their entries in $P$. Finally, putting $\\Lambda_A=\\bigotimes_{a\\in A}\\lambda_{p_a}$, we have $$\\label{eq:grouped-cell-locality}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Law}}(Y_{A,t}\\mid E,Y_{P,I},Y_{I,r\\cup s})\n =\\Lambda_A(\\,\\mathrm dY_{A,t}\\mid Y_{A,r},Y_{A,s}).$$ All these grouped assertions hold for every ordering of the three row groups and the three column groups.",
          "source_id": "PM-145.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "R3",
      "inputs": [
        "Saturation and the relative proper-plane product laws."
      ],
      "method": "Rolling an axis and conditioning through local summaries.",
      "output": "Exact square locality in every grouped orientation.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-square-locality"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-common-exact-lines",
      "depends_on": [
        "R3"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-common-exact-lines",
          "exact_quote": "For almost every parameter square $E$, the following assertions hold simultaneously at all individual cells.*\n\n1.  *The orthogonal projections onto $V_{a,j}$ and $W_{a,j}$ commute. Their common range $V_{a,j}\\cap W_{a,j}$ is spanned by the lines exact for both the full $k$-coordinate row law $\\lambda_{p_a}$ and the full $k$-coordinate column law $\\lambda_{q_j}$.*\n\n2.  *For distinct columns $s,t$, let $L^a_{s\\leftarrow t}(v)$ be the row Markov operator from cell $(a,t)$ to $(a,s)$, conditioned on the remaining $k-2$ row entries $v$. Then, for almost every $v$, $$P^W_{a,s}L^a_{s\\leftarrow t}(v)\n     =L^a_{s\\leftarrow t}(v)P^W_{a,t}.$$ Column operators likewise intertwine the corresponding row-active projections.*\n\n\n3.  *If $(\\chi_j)_{j\\in I}$ is an exact tuple for row $a$, with $\\prod_j\\chi_j(Y_{a,j})=c$, then $$\\label{eq:exact-partner-transport}\n     L^a_{s\\leftarrow t}(v)\\chi_t\n     =c\\!\\prod_{j\\ne s,t}\\overline{\\chi_j(v_j)}\\,\n       \\overline{\\chi_s}.$$ In particular, membership of one partner line in the column-active space is equivalent to membership of any other partner line in its column-active space. The analogous assertion holds with rows and columns exchanged.",
          "source_id": "PM-145.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "R4",
      "inputs": [
        "Square locality and parameter geometry."

      "output": "Common active spaces spanned by exact lines.",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-measurable-labels",
          "exact_quote": "There is a measurable countable family of abelian groups $u\\mapsto\\mathcal L(u)$, defined on a $\\nu$-conull set, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Each $\\ell\\in\\mathcal L(u)$ labels a line in $H_u$ with a measurable modulus-one representative $\\chi_{u,\\ell}$. The identity label $0$ has representative $1$. Distinct labels are orthogonal, every nonidentity representative has mean zero, and $$\\chi_{u,\\ell}\\chi_{u,\\ell'}\n     \\in\\mathbb T\\chi_{u,\\ell+\\ell'}.$$*\n\n2.  *For $\\pi$-almost every $p$, the exact lines at slot $i$ of $\\lambda_p$ are precisely $\\mathcal L(p_i)$, for every $i$, and they span its active space. Unique exact matching gives measurable group isomorphisms $$\\Phi_{st}(p):\\mathcal L(p_s)\\longrightarrow\\mathcal L(p_t)\n     \\qquad(s,t\\in I),$$ where $\\Phi_{ss}(p)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}$.*\n\n3.  *Local time gives measurable group isomorphisms $$\\tau_u:\\mathcal L(u)\\longrightarrow\\mathcal L(T_Uu),\n     \\qquad [\\chi]\\longmapsto[\\chi\\circ T_Y^{-1}].$$ They respect exact matching. All local time assertions may be imposed on an invariant conull set.*\n\n4.  *For $\\vartheta$-almost every parameter square, prescribing a label at any one cell gives a unique array of labels matched along every row and every column.",
          "source_id": "PM-145.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "R5",
      "inputs": [
        "Common exact lines and parameter geometry."
      ],
      "method": "Random-projection support, measurable spectral enumeration and descent to the local parameter.",
      "output": "Measurable local abelian label groups with consistent square matchings.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-measurable-labels"
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-blocks",
          "exact_quote": "There are a countable index set $\\mathcal B$, measurable partitions $$\\mathcal L(u)=\\bigsqcup_{b\\in\\mathcal B}B_b(u)\n \\quad\\text{for }\\nu\\text{-almost every }u,$$ and permutations $D_0,S_0$ of $\\mathcal B$ with these properties. Every $B_b(u)$ has a fixed finite positive cardinality $m_b$, independent of $u$. For one fixed pair $s\\ne t$, exact matching satisfies $$\\Phi_{st}(p)B_b(p_s)=B_{D_0b}(p_t)\n \\quad(\\pi\\text{-almost every }p),$$ and time satisfies $$\\tau_u B_b(u)=B_{S_0b}(T_Uu).$$ Moreover, there are a countable abelian group $\\mathcal L$, a partition $\\mathcal L=\\bigsqcup_b B_b$ into sets of cardinalities $m_b$, block-preserving group isomorphisms $J_u:\\mathcal L\\to\\mathcal L(u)$ for typical $u$, and an automorphism $G$ of $\\mathcal L$ such that $$\\label{eq:abstract-block-time}\n G^r B_b=B_{S_0^{-r}b}\\qquad(r\\in\\mathbb Z).$$ The isomorphisms $J_u$ are only asserted to exist separately in each fibre; they need not be chosen measurably or intertwine individual time trajectories.",
          "source_id": "PM-145.1"
        }

      "output": "Finite blocks with a time permutation realized by an abstract automorphism.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-finite-blocks"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-block-witness",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-block-witness",
          "exact_quote": "Let the saturated array object lie over the zero-entropy finite-alphabet mixing process $(X,\\mu_X,T_X)$ supplied by Proposition <a href=\"#prop:zero-entropy-witness\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:zero-entropy-witness\">2</a>, with its centered finite-valued one-site functions $f_1,\\ldots,f_k$ and nonzero level-$1$ product integral. There exist a function $f\\in\\{f_1,\\ldots,f_k\\}$, a countable abelian group $\\mathcal L$, an automorphism $G$ of $\\mathcal L$, a finite nonempty set $B\\subset\\mathcal L$, and a number $c>0$ with the following properties.*\n\n\n\n\n*For $\\nu$-almost every $u$, the probability space $(Y,\\eta_u)$ has factor map $x:Y\\to X$ with $x_*\\eta_u=\\mu_X$, and admits representatives $(\\chi_\\ell^u)_{\\ell\\in\\mathcal L}$ such that $$\\begin{gathered}\n \\chi_0^u=1,\\qquad |\\chi_\\ell^u|=1,\\qquad\n \\chi_\\ell^u\\chi_{\\ell'}^u\\in\\mathbb T\\chi_{\\ell+\\ell'}^u,\n \\label{eq:block-character-laws}\\\\\n \\int\\chi_\\ell^u\\,\\mathrm d\\eta_u=0\\quad(\\ell\\ne0),\\qquad\n \\langle \\chi_\\ell^u,\\chi_{\\ell'}^u\\rangle_{L^2(\\eta_u)}\n       =\\mathbf1_{\\{\\ell=\\ell'\\}}.\n \\nonumber\n\\end{gathered}$$ For every $r\\ge0$ the function $$\\label{eq:block-witness-sum}\n s_r(u)=\\sum_{\\ell\\in B}\n \\left|\\int f(T_X^r x(y))\\,\n               \\overline{\\chi_{G^r\\ell}^u(y)}\\,\\mathrm d\\eta_u(y)\\right|$$ has a measurable version and satisfies $$\\label{eq:block-witness-integral}\n 0\\le s_r(u)\\le |B|\\lVert f\\rVert_\\infty,\n \\qquad \\int_U s_r(u)\\,\\mathrm d\\nu(u)=c>0.$$ The group identifications used to choose the representatives in <a href=\"#eq:block-character-laws\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:block-character-laws\">[eq:block-character-laws]</a> need not be measurable in $u$. Their finite sums in <a href=\"#eq:block-witness-sum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:block-witness-sum\">[eq:block-witness-sum]</a> are intrinsic sums over the measurable moving blocks. In particular, no time invariance of an individual $\\eta_u$ is asserted or required.",
          "source_id": "PM-145.1"

For every centered finite-valued one-site function $f$ on $X$, every probability coupling $x:Y\\to X$, every family satisfying <a href=\"#eq:projective-characters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:projective-characters\">[eq:projective-characters]</a>, every automorphism $G$, and every $l\\in L$, one has $$\\label{eq:orbit-cancellation}\n \\lim_{N\\to\\infty}\\frac1N\\sum_{r=0}^{N-1}\n \\left|\\int f(T_X^r x)\\overline{\\chi_{G^r l}}\\,\\mathrm d\\eta\\right|=0.$$",

No standardness or countable-generation assumption is imposed on the original probability space.",

Then, for every fixed $k\\ge2$ and measurable $A_1,\\ldots,A_k$, $$\\mu\\left(\\bigcap_{i=1}^kT^{-t_i}A_i\\right)\n     \\longrightarrow\\prod_{i=1}^k\\mu(A_i)$$ along nonnegative integer times $t_1<\\cdots<t_k$ whose least successive gap tends to infinity. Only preimages under forward iterates are used.*\n\n2.  *Let $(S_t)_{t\\in\\mathbb R}$ be a probability-preserving group of invertible measurable transformations. Assume $\\mu(A\\cap S_t^{-1}B)\\to\\mu(A)\\mu(B)$ as $t\\to+\\infty$ for every $A,B\\in\\mathcal F$, and that its Koopman operators $U_t f=f\\circ S_t$ are strongly continuous on $L^1(\\mu)$; equivalently, $$\\lVert U_s1_A-1_A\\rVert_1\\longrightarrow0\\qquad(s\\to0)\n     \\quad\\text{for every }A\\in\\mathcal F.$$ The same product limit holds with $T^{-t_i}A_i$ replaced by $S_{t_i}^{-1}A_i$ and with arbitrary real times $t_1<\\cdots<t_k$ whose least successive gap tends to infinity.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **INV-MIXING**: Under invertibility, probability preservation and mixing, fixed measurable sets have the product limit along diverging positive-gap layouts.
- **INV-ARRAY-ORDER**: Proper last-axis planes are independent in the ordered array construction; row locality is obtained by extension arguments.
- **INV-FIBRE**: Cancellation uses the mixing process marginal on each fibre; time need not preserve an individual fibre law.
- **INV-ENTROPY**: Dissociated character cancellation permits arbitrary coupling when the finite-valued observable words have sublinear entropy.
- **INV-FLOW**: The flow conclusion requires probability preservation, mixing and strong continuity of the Koopman operators on the stated function space.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **ANTI-MULTIGENERATOR**: Apply the single-transformation conclusion to a mixing action with several time generators. -> Mixing with several generators can fail to imply higher-order mixing.
- **ANTI-SAMPLE**: Treat observed decay of finitely sampled correlations as a proof of the theorem hypotheses or as a uniform convergence rate. -> Finite observations do not establish the required asymptotic mixing hypothesis or provide an effective gap threshold.
- **ANTI-INDEPENDENCE**: Replace the full structural argument by pairwise independence of a joining. -> Residual higher-order interactions can remain.
- **ANTI-FIBRE**: Assume a conditional fibre law is invariant under time. -> Time transports the fibre to another parameter instead of necessarily preserving it.
- **ANTI-FLOW**: Apply the real-time conclusion using mixing alone without checking the continuity hypothesis. -> The approximation from real-time layouts to integer-time layouts lacks its stated continuity control.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The selected conclusion is an asymptotic product limit for arbitrary fixed measurable sets. The proof constructs ordered array laws using a free ultrafilter, obtains saturated extensions through sequential inverse limits, and extracts measurable countable families of exact lines. It supplies no finite input representation or terminating procedure for establishing the mixing hypotheses or deciding the asserted limit. Finite block cardinalities and polynomial orbit identities are intermediate structural conclusions, not a finite construction of the required systems or a finite certificate of the main conclusion.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **main-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The main conclusion requires an invertible mixing probability-preserving transformation. Blocked use: ['Applying the main theorem before establishing its dynamical hypotheses.']
- **fixed-events-diverging-gaps** (hypothesis): Higher-order mixing tests fixed measurable events along layouts whose least successive gap diverges. Blocked use: ['Replacing diverging gaps by a bounded-gap layout.', 'Allowing the tested events to vary with the layout without further justification.']
- **single-time-generator** (counterexample_scope): The auxiliary array axes do not extend the result to actions with several time generators. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to a mixing action with several generators.']
- **multidimensional-counterexample** (counterexample_scope): The introduction records a mixing multidimensional example that fails higher-order mixing. Blocked use: ['Using ordinary mixing alone to infer higher-order mixing for multidimensional actions.']
- **nonstandard-original-space** (edge_case): The original probability space need not be standard or countably generated; the subsequent conditional-law constructions are made after passing to standard spaces. Blocked use: ['Treating the absence of standardness assumptions on the original space as permission to use regular conditional laws on arbitrary spaces.']
- **extension-not-mixing** (edge_case): The saturated enlargement need not be mixing; its locality assertions use saturation and the array laws. Blocked use: ['Assuming that mixing of the original transformation implies mixing of the saturated enlargement.']
- **ordered-ultralimits** (nonconstructive): The initial array laws use a free ultrafilter and successive limits in a specified order. A finite procedure computing these laws is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Presenting the array construction as an executable finite algorithm.', 'Permuting the limit order without justification.']
- **dense-range-not-bounded-inverse** (edge_case): The operator argument uses dense-range cancellation and does not require bounded inverses of its positive parts. Blocked use: ['Replacing dense-range cancellation by an unjustified bounded inverse.']
- **pointwise-identifications** (nonconstructive): The abstract group identifications exist separately in each fibre; measurability and individual time intertwining are not asserted. Blocked use: ['Implementing a measurable global choice of those identifications from the existence statement.', 'Assuming a uniform time rule for labels inside a block.']
- **orbit-entropy-hypothesis** (hypothesis): Orbit cancellation assumes a mixing finite-alphabet stationary process with zero entropy rate. Its coupling need not make the fibre law invariant. Blocked use: ['Dropping zero entropy from the orbit-cancellation proposition.', 'Treating a conditional fibre as time invariant.']
- **polynomial-scope** (hypothesis): The polynomial cancellation lemma uses mixing and a bounded centered observable; it does not require zero entropy or invariance of the coupling law. Blocked use: ['Applying polynomial cancellation to an uncentered observable without controlling its mean.']
- **flow-continuity** (hypothesis): The flow consequence requires probability preservation, mixing and strong continuity of the Koopman operators on the stated function space. Blocked use: ['Applying the flow consequence without its continuity hypothesis.']
- **endomorphism-forward-preimages** (edge_case): The endomorphism consequence uses forward-iterate preimages and does not assert invertibility of the original transformation. Blocked use: ['Requiring inverse maps on the original endomorphism space.']
- **no-finite-rate** (unknown): A convergence rate, finite waiting-time certificate and finite decision procedure for mixing are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Treating a qualitative product limit as a finite-time independence guarantee.', 'Certifying the asymptotic hypotheses from a finite trajectory alone.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 145 (`lean/docs/145.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Rokhlin's multiple-mixing problem for one transformation](../../preprints/Rokhlins-multiple-mixing-problem-for-one-transformation-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> Rokhlin's multiple-mixing problem asks whether ordinary mixing of one invertible probability-preserving transformation implies mixing of every finite order. The formalization proves this implication. For each $k\ge3$ and every $k$ measurable sets, the measure of their translated intersection tends to the product of their measures as all successive time gaps tend to infinity.

| declaration | kind |

| `timeMap` | def |
| `IsMixing` | def |

Comparator source `Rokhlin.lean` (1190 bytes, sha256 `f1cb24d9b04f86273fd9895eef73da12dcda912a6c9e1fb90956ed6af2a8383d`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

open MeasureTheory Filter
open scoped BigOperators Topology

namespace Rokhlin

variable {Ω : Type*} [MeasurableSpace Ω]

def timeMap (T : Ω ≃ᵐ Ω) (n : ℤ) : Ω → Ω :=
  ⇑((T.toEquiv : Equiv.Perm Ω) ^ n)

def IsMixing (μ : Measure Ω) (T : Ω ≃ᵐ Ω) : Prop :=
  ∀ A B : Set Ω, MeasurableSet A → MeasurableSet B →
    Tendsto (fun n : ℤ => μ (A ∩ timeMap T n ⁻¹' B))
      (comap Int.natAbs atTop) (𝓝 (μ A * μ B))

def layoutTime {k : ℕ} (gaps : Fin (k - 1) → ℕ+) (i : Fin k) : ℕ :=
  ∑ j : Fin (k - 1), if j.val < i.val then (gaps j : ℕ) else 0

def MixingOfOrder (μ : Measure Ω) (T : Ω ≃ᵐ Ω) (k : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ A : Fin k → Set Ω, (∀ i, MeasurableSet (A i)) →
    Tendsto
      (fun gaps : Fin (k - 1) → ℕ+ =>
        μ (⋂ i : Fin k, timeMap T (layoutTime gaps i : ℤ) ⁻¹' A i))
      atTop (𝓝 (∏ i : Fin k, μ (A i)))

theorem mixing_all_finite_orders (μ : Measure Ω) [IsProbabilityMeasure μ]
    (T : Ω ≃ᵐ Ω) (h_pres : MeasurePreserving T μ μ) (h_mix : IsMixing μ T) :
    ∀ k : ℕ, 3 ≤ k → MixingOfOrder μ T k := by
  sorry

end Rokhlin

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Every invertible mixing probability-preserving transformation is mixing of every finite order. Thus Rokhlin's multiple-mixing problem for a single transformation has an affirmative answer.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $(\Omega,\mathcal F,\mu)$ be a probability space and let $T$ be an invertible measurable transformation with measurable inverse that preserves $\mu$. The transformation is *mixing* if $$\mu(A\cap T^{-n}B)\longrightarrow\mu(A)\mu(B)
 \qquad(|n|\to\infty)$$ for every $A,B\in\mathcal F$. For an integer $k\ge2$, it is *mixing of order $k$* if $$\mu\left(\bigcap_{i=1}^k T^{-t_i}A_i\right)
 \longrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^k\mu(A_i)$$ whenever $t_1<\cdots<t_k$ are integers and $\min_{i<k}(t_{i+1}-t_i)\to\infty$, for every fixed choice of the measurable sets $A_i$. Invariance allows us to take $t_1=0$.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Every invertible mixing probability-preserving transformation is mixing of every finite order. More explicitly, under the assumptions above, for every $k\ge3$ and every $A_1,\ldots,A_k\in\mathcal F$, $$\mu\bigl(A_1\cap T^{-n_1}A_2\cap\cdots\cap
             T^{-(n_1+\cdots+n_{k-1})}A_k\bigr)
 \longrightarrow\prod_{i=1}^k\mu(A_i)$$ as $\min(n_1,\ldots,n_{k-1})\to\infty$ through positive integers. No standardness or countable-generation assumption is imposed on the original probability space.*

</div>

Corollary <a href="#cor:endomorphisms-flows" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:endomorphisms-flows">27</a> extends the conclusion to mixing endomorphisms on arbitrary probability spaces and to mixing flows whose Koopman operators are strongly continuous on $L^1$. These consequences are stated and proved in Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">8</a>.

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> answers Rokhlin’s multiple-mixing problem affirmatively for one transformation. Rokhlin (Rokhlin 1949) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Rokhlin1949](#ref-Rokhlin1949)] introduced higher-order mixing and proved it for ergodic surjective continuous endomorphisms of compact commutative groups with Haar probability. The question whether ordinary mixing alone implies the next order is presented as Rokhlin’s 1949 question in de la Rue’s account (Rue 2006, sec. 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[deLaRue2006](#ref-deLaRue2006)]; Ryzhikov surveys the all-orders formulation in (Valery V. Ryzhikov 2024, sec. 1) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Ryzhikov2024](#ref-Ryzhikov2024)]. We count the order by the number of sets, as de la Rue does: order $k$ here corresponds to multiplicity $k-1$ in Rokhlin and Ryzhikov. Neither a group structure nor any other special form of the transformation is assumed in our theorem.

The distinction between one time generator and several is essential. Ledrappier’s zero-entropy, mixing $\mathbb Z^2$ example is not threefold mixing (Ledrappier 1978) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Ledrappier1978](#ref-Ledrappier1978)]; de la Rue explains its three-dot relation and why a direct one-dimensional analogue fails (Rue 2006, sec. 1.2 and 3.4, preprint version) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[deLaRue2006](#ref-deLaRue2006)]. On the positive side, Kalikow proved the twofold-to-threefold implication for rank-one transformations (Kalikow 1984) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Kalikow1984](#ref-Kalikow1984)]. Host obtained all orders under a singular-spectrum hypothesis (Host 1991) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Host1991](#ref-Host1991)], and Ryzhikov obtained all-orders conclusions for mixing finite-rank automorphisms (V. V. Ryzhikov 1993) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Ryzhikov1993](#ref-Ryzhikov1993)]. More recently, Kanigowski and Ravotti proved that mixing locally uniformly shearing flows are mixing of all orders (Kanigowski and Ravotti 2024, Theorem 6.8) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[KanigowskiRavotti2024](#ref-KanigowskiRavotti2024)]. These results require structure not present in a general mixing transformation. For strongly mixing actions of countable abelian groups on separable probability spaces, Bergelson and Zelada proved that the higher-order good layouts form a strong Ramsey-large set (V. Bergelson and Zelada 2024, Theorem 1.11) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BergelsonZelada2024](#ref-BergelsonZelada2024)]; this does not assert convergence along every diverging-gap layout required here.

The language of joinings, originating with Furstenberg’s work on disjointness (Furstenberg 1967) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Furstenberg1967](#ref-Furstenberg1967)], makes a failure of higher-order mixing visible as a nonproduct limit law with independent proper marginals. Pairwise independence alone does not force a joining to be product. For an aperiodic finite-alphabet process, Janvresse and de la Rue show that a pairwise-independent joining in which one coordinate is a continuous function of the other two forces positive entropy (Janvresse and Rue 2008) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[JanvresseDeLaRue2008](#ref-JanvresseDeLaRue2008)]. For the twofold/threefold question, de la Rue records both the limit joining and a zero-entropy reduction (Rue 2006, Propositions 3.1 and 3.2, preprint version) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[deLaRue2006](#ref-deLaRue2006)]. At arbitrary order, an antecedent Pinsker-factor reduction is described by Ryzhikov (V. V. Ryzhikov 1997, 251) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Ryzhikov1997](#ref-Ryzhikov1997)], who attributes it to a lemma of Thouvenot (Thouvenot 1975) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Thouvenot1975Pinsker](#ref-Thouvenot1975Pinsker)]. We prove below the exact finite-alphabet, least-failure form needed here, including the passage from a possibly nonstandard original probability space. The new obstacle after that reduction is to control all residual interactions of the proper-independent limit law, not merely a selected joining. We therefore use an ordered family of array laws and prove the later operator and cancellation steps directly. Markov-operator methods for joinings and intertwinings provide a related precedent (V. V. Ryzhikov 1994, 1997) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Ryzhikov1994](#ref-Ryzhikov1994), [Ryzhikov1997](#ref-Ryzhikov1997)]; the rectangle and exact-line statements used here are established under our specific hypotheses.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The structural argument

Suppose that $k$ is the least failing order, and put $I=\{1,\ldots,k\}$.
